        Idea:
Implement a human-like Discord conversation pacing system for Veas. It should combine deterministic programmatic signals and LLM judgement so the bot feels like a real attentive person rather than message-in/reply-out. Requirements: handle Discord typing signals and pause/extend while user is composing; coalesce multi-message bursts and respond to the whole thought rather than every line; support wait/react/silence/answer decisions before the full agentic turn; use tasteful/elegant emoji/reaction behavior sparingly; avoid over-questioning and compulsive replies; preserve safety/OOB behavior; make typing indicators feel human, including possible stop/start and not typing while the user is typing; add observability/reasoning for why the pacer waited/reacted/silenced/answered; make timing configurable and per-user preference friendly; add tests and docs. Go deep on UX details and implement the best pragmatic version in this repo.

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Human-Like Discord Conversation Pacing

## Overview
Veas already has the right core surfaces: Discord gateway ingestion in `app/services/discord.py`, generic inbound normalization in `app/services/inbound.py`, fixed burst coalescing in `app/services/debouncer.py`, the two-phase agentic lifecycle in `app/services/agentic.py`, outbound/OOB delivery in `app/services/messaging.py`, media enqueue paths in `app/services/transcription.py` and `app/services/vision.py`, startup wiring in `app/main.py`, and recovery in `app/services/recovery.py`.

The latest critique does not show a wrong root cause or wrong architecture. The root cause is still that Discord has fixed burst coalescing plus immediate/provider-level typing, with no pre-turn pacing policy. The remaining concrete gap is source propagation: Discord catch-up enters through `process_inbound(...)`, so `source="catch_up"` will not reach `coalescer.add(...)` unless `process_inbound` accepts and forwards source metadata. The typing callback API also needs implementation-time verification, but that is a bounded Discord transport task rather than a reason to change approach.

The implementation should add a Discord-first pacing layer before the full agentic turn. Deterministic signals handle obvious wait/answer safety cases, a small spend-accounted LLM judgement handles ambiguous routine/notable cases, and substantive text remains in the existing agentic/OOB-protected path. Wait/react/silence/answer decisions get durable observability even when no `bot_turns` row is created.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Configuration, Preferences, Observability

### Step 1: Add global pacing settings (`app/config.py`, `.env.example`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Add conservative Discord pacing settings:
   - `discord_pacing_enabled: bool = True`
   - `discord_pacing_idle_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_max_wait_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_typing_hold_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_min_reply_delay_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_max_reply_delay_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_llm_enabled: bool = True`
   - `discord_pacing_model`, defaulting to the existing cheap judgement model unless product tuning chooses otherwise.
2. Gate behavior on `messaging_provider == "discord"` so WhatsApp/meta behavior stays unchanged.
3. Add bounded min/max validation in code so per-user overrides cannot create extreme sleeps.

### Step 2: Add per-user pacing preferences (`migrations/0008_discord_pacing.sql`, `app/models/user.py`, `tests/conftest.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `users.pacing_preferences jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb` in the new migration.
2. Define supported preference keys in code: `idle_seconds`, `max_wait_seconds`, `typing_hold_seconds`, `llm_pacing_enabled`, `reaction_style`, and `answer_bias`.
3. Implement a resolver that merges global settings with `users.pacing_preferences`, clamps unsafe values, and falls back cleanly for users with `{}`.
4. Update `FakePool` user rows and user fetch helpers so tests can store and read pacing preferences.

### Step 3: Create durable pacing observability (`migrations/0008_discord_pacing.sql`, `tests/conftest.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a `pacing_events` table with `user_id`, `message_ids uuid[]`, `provider`, `decision`, `reason`, `deterministic_signals jsonb`, `llm_judgment jsonb`, `preferences_snapshot jsonb`, `source`, `created_at`, and optional `acted_at`.
2. Add indexes for recent events by user/time and message ids.
3. Update `FakePool` to support inserting pacing events and reading them in tests.
4. Record events for every terminal decision (`react`, `silence`, `answer`) and meaningful waits/extensions.

## Phase 2: Pacer Core And Cost Controls

### Step 4: Implement the pacer module (`app/services/pacer.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Define `PacingDecision` values: `wait`, `react`, `silence`, and `answer`.
2. Define `PacingSignals` from real message rows and runtime state: burst size, elapsed time, last inbound age, max charge, current Discord typing state, source (`live`, `media`, `recovery`, `catch_up`), media presence, question/direct-request indicators, fragment-like content, repeat reactions, and provider.
3. Fetch message rows by `message_ids` inside the pacer so text, charge, media type, and stale/recovered status are based on persisted data rather than caller guesses.
4. Add deterministic hard gates:
   - `/pause` and `/resume` remain handled before pacing in `app/services/inbound.py`.
   - `crisis` always answers through the full agentic path, with no silence or reaction-only decision.
   - `charged` may wait briefly for continuation but cannot be reaction-only; silence requires explicit LLM justification and a recorded event.
   - Media-derived messages from transcription/vision answer after coalescing and should not be pre-turn reacted/silenced.
   - Recovered or catch-up messages bypass human typing/reaction behavior and answer immediately or enter the existing recovery flow.
   - If Discord says the user is typing, wait/extend up to the configured cap.
5. Enforce reaction taste in code: small allowlist, cooldown by recent `pacing_events`, no reactions for crisis/media/recovered/catch-up messages, and no repeated reaction-only responses in a short window.

### Step 5: Add spend-accounted LLM pacing judgement (`app/services/pacer.py`, `app/services/spend.py` or a shared cost helper)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Before any pre-turn LLM call, check `is_under_cap(pool, "text")`; if not under cap, skip LLM pacing and choose deterministic `answer` or defer only through the existing spend-cap behavior.
2. After the LLM judgement call, record cost with the same pricing rules used by agentic turns. Prefer extracting a shared helper from `app/services/agentic.py` rather than duplicating private cost math.
3. Use a JSON-only prompt for ambiguous routine/notable live Discord bursts. Allowed output fields: `decision`, `reason`, `emoji`, and `confidence`.
4. Validate the LLM decision against deterministic gates before acting. Invalid or unsafe judgement falls back to `answer` and records the fallback reason.

## Phase 3: Source Propagation, Coalescer, And Agentic Metadata

### Step 6: Add source metadata to inbound processing (`app/services/inbound.py`, `app/services/discord.py`, tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `process_inbound(pool, payload, coalescer=None)` with a keyword-only parameter such as `coalescer_source: str = "live"`.
2. Forward `coalescer_source` into every text/document coalescer enqueue as `await coalescer.add(user.id, row["id"], user, source=coalescer_source)`.
3. Keep the default as `"live"` so WhatsApp webhooks, Twilio webhooks, and normal Discord gateway messages preserve current behavior without caller changes.
4. In `app/services/discord.py`, call `process_inbound(..., coalescer_source="catch_up")` from `catch_up_recent_messages(...)`.
5. Add tests proving catch-up ingestion through `process_inbound(...)` reaches the coalescer with `source="catch_up"`, while normal inbound calls default to `source="live"`.

### Step 7: Extend burst coalescing without breaking legacy callers (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep the existing `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)` path intact for tests and non-paced use.
2. Add optional constructor dependencies: `pacer`, `on_paced_burst_complete`, and default `source="live"` support for `add(...)`.
3. Keep `add(user_id, message_id, user)` backward compatible; add optional keyword metadata such as `source="live"` so inbound, recovery, media, and catch-up callers can be explicit.
4. Store source metadata per burst. If multiple sources join one burst, preserve the highest-safety source in order such as `recovery`/`catch_up` over `media` over `live`.
5. On idle expiry, if no pacer is configured, call the legacy callback exactly as today.
6. If a pacer is configured, ask it for a decision. For `wait`, reschedule the same burst without losing message ids. For `answer`, call `on_paced_burst_complete(message_ids, user, pacing_metadata=...)`. For `react`/`silence`, mark messages processed and do not run the full agentic turn.
7. Preserve `add_burst(...)` as direct callback behavior for crash recovery of already-formed batches.

### Step 8: Add an agentic metadata entry point (`app/services/agentic.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata(triggering_message_ids, user, *, pacing_metadata)` as the explicit callback target for paced answers.
2. Delegate to `_run_agentic(..., trigger_metadata={"kind": "inbound", "pacing": pacing_metadata})` so Phase A sees why the pacer answered.
3. Keep `run_agentic_turn(...)` unchanged for current callers.
4. In the Phase A seed, include compact pacing context: decision reason, wait count/duration, user typing stopped/expired, burst size, source, and any preference override that mattered.
5. Tighten prompt language around whole-burst response, avoiding over-questioning, sparse reactions, and acceptable silence while preserving the existing OOB rules.

### Step 9: Wire the live app construction (`app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Construct a `DiscordPacer` or equivalent only when `messaging_provider == "discord"` and pacing is enabled.
2. Pass `pool`, `settings`, the pacer, and `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata` into `BurstCoalescer` during startup.
3. Store the pacer on `app.state.discord_pacer` so `DiscordGatewayBot`, recovery, and tests can share typing state.
4. Keep the old `BurstCoalescer(on_burst_complete=run_agentic_turn)` construction for non-Discord or pacing-disabled providers.
5. Add startup tests or app-lifespan tests proving the production construction actually attaches the pacer for Discord.

## Phase 4: Discord Typing And Delivery

### Step 10: Verify and wire Discord typing-start state (`app/services/discord.py`, `tests/test_discord.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. During implementation, verify the exact `resident_chat_runtime.discord_gateway.GatewayCallbacks` API by inspecting the installed package in the runnable project environment.
2. If it supports typing callbacks, register `on_typing_start`; otherwise, detect `TYPING_START` in `DiscordGatewayBot._run_once` before or around `dispatch_payload`.
3. Add a test for whichever path is implemented: native callback dispatch or raw `TYPING_START` fallback.
4. On typing start, update the pacer’s per-user/channel typing timestamp and do not enqueue a message.
5. Remove the current unconditional `_send_typing_after_delay(...)` task from `_handle_message`; the pacer owns typing UX.
6. Add tests that a partner message is ingested without immediate typing and that typing-start events update pacer state.

### Step 11: Parameterize low-level Discord typing (`app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/messaging.py`, `app/services/agentic.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Change `discord.send_text(to, body)` to accept a keyword such as `send_typing_indicator: bool = True`.
2. Change `send_outbound(...)` to accept a matching Discord typing option and pass it through only for the Discord provider.
3. When an outbound is sent from a paced agentic turn, pass `send_typing_indicator=False` because the pacer already handled typing timing.
4. Leave default low-level typing enabled for non-paced Discord sends such as pause confirmations or operational messages, unless a caller explicitly opts out.
5. Add tests proving paced sends do not call `send_typing` twice and non-paced Discord sends still can emit typing.

### Step 12: Make answer typing feel human (`app/services/pacer.py`, `app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After the pacer chooses `answer`, delay within configured min/max bounds before starting typing.
2. Suppress typing while the user is still typing; if they resume typing during the answer delay, wait until the typing hold expires or the max wait cap is reached.
3. For longer answer generation, optionally refresh Discord typing once using the existing REST typing endpoint; do not loop indefinitely.
4. Record typing wait/started/suppressed facts in `pacing_events.deterministic_signals` or associated reason text.

## Phase 5: Media, Recovery, And Catch-Up Paths

### Step 13: Make non-text media paths explicit (`app/services/transcription.py`, `app/services/vision.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Pass `source="media"` when transcription or vision enqueues a processed inbound message into the coalescer.
2. Ensure media-derived bursts can coalesce with nearby text from the same user, but cannot be pre-turn silenced or reaction-only.
3. Include media indicators in `PacingSignals` so the answer path can tell Phase A that the trigger included voice/image/document context.
4. Add tests where voice/image analysis joins a text burst and produces one answer decision.

### Step 14: Make recovery and catch-up behavior explicit (`app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep `coalescer.add_burst(...)` as direct recovery behavior for known crashed grouped turns.
2. Pass `source="recovery"` for old raw messages recovered by `recover_on_startup(...)`; the pacer should answer without live typing/reaction/silence behavior.
3. Pass `coalescer_source="catch_up"` into `process_inbound(...)` from `catch_up_recent_messages(...)`; do not rely on direct coalescer access in catch-up because catch-up uses the generic inbound processor.
4. Ensure the pacer treats `source="catch_up"` as stale offline history: no reaction-only decision, no silence decision, and no human typing indicators.
5. Add tests proving recovered and catch-up raw messages are not delayed by user-typing state and do not trigger reactions.

## Phase 6: Tests And Docs

### Step 15: Add focused pacing tests (`tests/test_pacer.py`, `tests/test_debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Test deterministic decisions: crisis answers, typing waits, max wait eventually answers, media answers, recovery answers without typing/reaction, catch-up answers without typing/reaction, and unsupported providers use legacy behavior.
2. Test LLM judgement paths with mocked responses, including invalid judgement fallback and spend-cap skip.
3. Test per-user preferences override global timing within bounds.
4. Test observability for wait/react/silence/answer, including signals, reason, preferences snapshot, and source.

### Step 16: Update integration-adjacent tests (`tests/test_discord.py`, `tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_send_outbound.py`, `tests/test_oob_check.py`, `tests/test_whatsapp_webhook.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update Discord tests to assert inbound messages do not emit immediate typing.
2. Add typing-start gateway tests for the verified callback or raw-event path.
3. Add catch-up tests proving `process_inbound(..., coalescer_source="catch_up")` forwards source metadata into the coalescer.
4. Add lifecycle tests proving pacing metadata reaches Phase A and paced outbound disables duplicate low-level typing.
5. Add send-outbound tests for `send_typing_indicator=False` on Discord and default behavior for non-paced sends.
6. Keep OOB tests passing unchanged, proving substantive text still goes through existing outbound guards.
7. Keep WhatsApp webhook tests passing, proving default `coalescer_source="live"` does not alter existing generic inbound behavior.

### Step 17: Document pacing behavior (`README.md` or a focused docs section)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document wait/react/silence/answer decisions, config knobs, per-user preference JSON keys, and defaults.
2. Explain that pre-turn LLM judgement is spend-accounted and disabled when the text spend cap is hit.
3. Add operator tuning guidance for idle/max wait, typing hold, reaction sparseness, disabling pacing, and how catch-up/recovery differ from live messages.

## Execution Order
1. Add settings, per-user preference storage, and `pacing_events` first so behavior has a stable contract.
2. Implement source propagation in `process_inbound(...)` before relying on catch-up/media/recovery source semantics.
3. Implement `app/services/pacer.py` with unit tests before wiring it into live runtime.
4. Add spend accounting for LLM judgement before enabling any LLM pacing path.
5. Extend `BurstCoalescer` with backward-compatible paced callbacks and source metadata.
6. Add `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata(...)` and wire `app/main.py` so production can actually use the pacer.
7. Parameterize Discord typing in `discord.send_text` and `send_outbound` before moving typing ownership to the pacer.
8. Verify and wire gateway typing-start events, then remove immediate inbound typing.
9. Cover media, recovery, and catch-up sources.
10. Finish with docs and broad regression tests.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused unit tests first: `pytest tests/test_pacer.py tests/test_debouncer.py`.
2. Run inbound/source propagation tests: `pytest tests/test_discord.py tests/test_whatsapp_webhook.py`.
3. Run outbound and lifecycle tests: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py`.
4. Run safety tests: `pytest tests/test_oob_check.py`.
5. Run media and recovery tests: `pytest tests/test_recovery.py tests/test_media.py`.
6. Run the full suite with the project’s normal test command once focused checks pass.

        Settled decisions (verify the executor implemented these correctly):
- discord-first-scope: Implement pacing for Discord first; keep WhatsApp/meta and other providers on existing behavior unless explicitly enabled later.
- pre-turn-pacer-boundary: The pacer decides wait/react/silence/answer before the agentic turn, but substantive text remains inside the existing agentic and OOB-protected outbound path.
- source-metadata-contract: Every paced coalescer entry carries source metadata; generic inbound defaults to `live`, while catch-up, recovery, transcription, and vision pass explicit sources.
- per-user-preferences: Per-user preference friendliness means bounded JSON preferences stored on `users`, merged with global settings and tested; no user-facing preference editor is required for this version.
- stale-history-behavior: Recovered and catch-up messages do not receive live typing, reaction-only handling, or silence decisions.
- typing-ownership: Paced Discord answers own typing timing in the pacer; low-level Discord sends must support suppressing their default typing indicator to avoid duplication.

Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: Recovery path: `app/services/recovery.py` calls both `coalescer.add_burst(...)` for crashed turns and `coalescer.add(...)` for old raw messages. The plan correctly notes that `add_burst` should keep direct recovery behavior, but it does not say whether recovered raw Discord messages should bypass pacing or receive a special recovered/offline signal to avoid delayed typing/reaction behavior for stale messages.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "verified"
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: The revised plan updates source metadata at `coalescer.add(...)` call sites, but catch-up source still crosses through `process_inbound(...)`, a supporting integration location not explicitly named in the catch-up step. This is the same concrete source propagation risk from the scope check.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "verified"
  },
  {
    "id": "callers",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: Callback signature: `BurstCoalescer` currently expects `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)` and its callers include `app/main.py`, tests, recovery, inbound text, transcription, and vision. The plan wants to pass pacing metadata into Phase A, but `run_agentic_turn` exposes no metadata parameter today, so implementation needs either a new callback signature, a wrapper callback, or a new `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata` instead of only changing `_run_agentic` internals.",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "verified"
  },
  {
    "id": "conventions",
    "concern": "Does the approach match how the codebase solves similar problems?: LLM provider convention: The codebase's conversational, scoring, and OOB LLM paths currently use Anthropic models and record cost through `app/services/spend.py`. The plan says to add `discord_pacing_model` defaulting to the cheap judgement model, but it does not explicitly require using the existing spend cap/cost accounting path for the pacer LLM calls; that could introduce untracked cost and behavior outside existing operational controls.",
    "severity": "minor",
    "status": "verified"
  },

  },

    "status": "verified"

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion has `requires` capabilities that are not satisfiable by container workers (e.g., `drive_browser`, `subjective_judgment`), mark it `deferred_human` — NOT `fail` or `waived`. Deferred-human criteria do NOT count toward `needs_rework`.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.
        - The decisions listed above were settled at the gate stage. Verify that the executor implemented each settled decision correctly. Flag deviations from these decisions, but do not question the decisions themselves.
        - baseline_test_failures in finalize.json lists tests that were already failing before execution. Do not flag these as rework items unless the executor introduced new failures in those same tests.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`
          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.

        Idea:
Implement a human-like Discord conversation pacing system for Veas. It should combine deterministic programmatic signals and LLM judgement so the bot feels like a real attentive person rather than message-in/reply-out. Requirements: handle Discord typing signals and pause/extend while user is composing; coalesce multi-message bursts and respond to the whole thought rather than every line; support wait/react/silence/answer decisions before the full agentic turn; use tasteful/elegant emoji/reaction behavior sparingly; avoid over-questioning and compulsive replies; preserve safety/OOB behavior; make typing indicators feel human, including possible stop/start and not typing while the user is typing; add observability/reasoning for why the pacer waited/reacted/silenced/answered; make timing configurable and per-user preference friendly; add tests and docs. Go deep on UX details and implement the best pragmatic version in this repo.

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Human-Like Discord Conversation Pacing

## Overview
Veas already has the right core surfaces: Discord gateway ingestion in `app/services/discord.py`, generic inbound normalization in `app/services/inbound.py`, fixed burst coalescing in `app/services/debouncer.py`, the two-phase agentic lifecycle in `app/services/agentic.py`, outbound/OOB delivery in `app/services/messaging.py`, media enqueue paths in `app/services/transcription.py` and `app/services/vision.py`, startup wiring in `app/main.py`, and recovery in `app/services/recovery.py`.

The latest critique does not show a wrong root cause or wrong architecture. The root cause is still that Discord has fixed burst coalescing plus immediate/provider-level typing, with no pre-turn pacing policy. The remaining concrete gap is source propagation: Discord catch-up enters through `process_inbound(...)`, so `source="catch_up"` will not reach `coalescer.add(...)` unless `process_inbound` accepts and forwards source metadata. The typing callback API also needs implementation-time verification, but that is a bounded Discord transport task rather than a reason to change approach.

The implementation should add a Discord-first pacing layer before the full agentic turn. Deterministic signals handle obvious wait/answer safety cases, a small spend-accounted LLM judgement handles ambiguous routine/notable cases, and substantive text remains in the existing agentic/OOB-protected path. Wait/react/silence/answer decisions get durable observability even when no `bot_turns` row is created.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Configuration, Preferences, Observability

### Step 1: Add global pacing settings (`app/config.py`, `.env.example`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Add conservative Discord pacing settings:
   - `discord_pacing_enabled: bool = True`
   - `discord_pacing_idle_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_max_wait_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_typing_hold_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_min_reply_delay_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_max_reply_delay_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_llm_enabled: bool = True`
   - `discord_pacing_model`, defaulting to the existing cheap judgement model unless product tuning chooses otherwise.
2. Gate behavior on `messaging_provider == "discord"` so WhatsApp/meta behavior stays unchanged.
3. Add bounded min/max validation in code so per-user overrides cannot create extreme sleeps.

### Step 2: Add per-user pacing preferences (`migrations/0008_discord_pacing.sql`, `app/models/user.py`, `tests/conftest.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `users.pacing_preferences jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb` in the new migration.
2. Define supported preference keys in code: `idle_seconds`, `max_wait_seconds`, `typing_hold_seconds`, `llm_pacing_enabled`, `reaction_style`, and `answer_bias`.
3. Implement a resolver that merges global settings with `users.pacing_preferences`, clamps unsafe values, and falls back cleanly for users with `{}`.
4. Update `FakePool` user rows and user fetch helpers so tests can store and read pacing preferences.

### Step 3: Create durable pacing observability (`migrations/0008_discord_pacing.sql`, `tests/conftest.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a `pacing_events` table with `user_id`, `message_ids uuid[]`, `provider`, `decision`, `reason`, `deterministic_signals jsonb`, `llm_judgment jsonb`, `preferences_snapshot jsonb`, `source`, `created_at`, and optional `acted_at`.
2. Add indexes for recent events by user/time and message ids.
3. Update `FakePool` to support inserting pacing events and reading them in tests.
4. Record events for every terminal decision (`react`, `silence`, `answer`) and meaningful waits/extensions.

## Phase 2: Pacer Core And Cost Controls

### Step 4: Implement the pacer module (`app/services/pacer.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Define `PacingDecision` values: `wait`, `react`, `silence`, and `answer`.
2. Define `PacingSignals` from real message rows and runtime state: burst size, elapsed time, last inbound age, max charge, current Discord typing state, source (`live`, `media`, `recovery`, `catch_up`), media presence, question/direct-request indicators, fragment-like content, repeat reactions, and provider.
3. Fetch message rows by `message_ids` inside the pacer so text, charge, media type, and stale/recovered status are based on persisted data rather than caller guesses.
4. Add deterministic hard gates:
   - `/pause` and `/resume` remain handled before pacing in `app/services/inbound.py`.
   - `crisis` always answers through the full agentic path, with no silence or reaction-only decision.
   - `charged` may wait briefly for continuation but cannot be reaction-only; silence requires explicit LLM justification and a recorded event.
   - Media-derived messages from transcription/vision answer after coalescing and should not be pre-turn reacted/silenced.
   - Recovered or catch-up messages bypass human typing/reaction behavior and answer immediately or enter the existing recovery flow.
   - If Discord says the user is typing, wait/extend up to the configured cap.
5. Enforce reaction taste in code: small allowlist, cooldown by recent `pacing_events`, no reactions for crisis/media/recovered/catch-up messages, and no repeated reaction-only responses in a short window.

### Step 5: Add spend-accounted LLM pacing judgement (`app/services/pacer.py`, `app/services/spend.py` or a shared cost helper)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Before any pre-turn LLM call, check `is_under_cap(pool, "text")`; if not under cap, skip LLM pacing and choose deterministic `answer` or defer only through the existing spend-cap behavior.
2. After the LLM judgement call, record cost with the same pricing rules used by agentic turns. Prefer extracting a shared helper from `app/services/agentic.py` rather than duplicating private cost math.
3. Use a JSON-only prompt for ambiguous routine/notable live Discord bursts. Allowed output fields: `decision`, `reason`, `emoji`, and `confidence`.
4. Validate the LLM decision against deterministic gates before acting. Invalid or unsafe judgement falls back to `answer` and records the fallback reason.

## Phase 3: Source Propagation, Coalescer, And Agentic Metadata

### Step 6: Add source metadata to inbound processing (`app/services/inbound.py`, `app/services/discord.py`, tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `process_inbound(pool, payload, coalescer=None)` with a keyword-only parameter such as `coalescer_source: str = "live"`.
2. Forward `coalescer_source` into every text/document coalescer enqueue as `await coalescer.add(user.id, row["id"], user, source=coalescer_source)`.
3. Keep the default as `"live"` so WhatsApp webhooks, Twilio webhooks, and normal Discord gateway messages preserve current behavior without caller changes.
4. In `app/services/discord.py`, call `process_inbound(..., coalescer_source="catch_up")` from `catch_up_recent_messages(...)`.
5. Add tests proving catch-up ingestion through `process_inbound(...)` reaches the coalescer with `source="catch_up"`, while normal inbound calls default to `source="live"`.

### Step 7: Extend burst coalescing without breaking legacy callers (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep the existing `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)` path intact for tests and non-paced use.
2. Add optional constructor dependencies: `pacer`, `on_paced_burst_complete`, and default `source="live"` support for `add(...)`.
3. Keep `add(user_id, message_id, user)` backward compatible; add optional keyword metadata such as `source="live"` so inbound, recovery, media, and catch-up callers can be explicit.
4. Store source metadata per burst. If multiple sources join one burst, preserve the highest-safety source in order such as `recovery`/`catch_up` over `media` over `live`.
5. On idle expiry, if no pacer is configured, call the legacy callback exactly as today.
6. If a pacer is configured, ask it for a decision. For `wait`, reschedule the same burst without losing message ids. For `answer`, call `on_paced_burst_complete(message_ids, user, pacing_metadata=...)`. For `react`/`silence`, mark messages processed and do not run the full agentic turn.
7. Preserve `add_burst(...)` as direct callback behavior for crash recovery of already-formed batches.

### Step 8: Add an agentic metadata entry point (`app/services/agentic.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata(triggering_message_ids, user, *, pacing_metadata)` as the explicit callback target for paced answers.
2. Delegate to `_run_agentic(..., trigger_metadata={"kind": "inbound", "pacing": pacing_metadata})` so Phase A sees why the pacer answered.
3. Keep `run_agentic_turn(...)` unchanged for current callers.
4. In the Phase A seed, include compact pacing context: decision reason, wait count/duration, user typing stopped/expired, burst size, source, and any preference override that mattered.
5. Tighten prompt language around whole-burst response, avoiding over-questioning, sparse reactions, and acceptable silence while preserving the existing OOB rules.

### Step 9: Wire the live app construction (`app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Construct a `DiscordPacer` or equivalent only when `messaging_provider == "discord"` and pacing is enabled.
2. Pass `pool`, `settings`, the pacer, and `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata` into `BurstCoalescer` during startup.
3. Store the pacer on `app.state.discord_pacer` so `DiscordGatewayBot`, recovery, and tests can share typing state.
4. Keep the old `BurstCoalescer(on_burst_complete=run_agentic_turn)` construction for non-Discord or pacing-disabled providers.
5. Add startup tests or app-lifespan tests proving the production construction actually attaches the pacer for Discord.

## Phase 4: Discord Typing And Delivery

### Step 10: Verify and wire Discord typing-start state (`app/services/discord.py`, `tests/test_discord.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. During implementation, verify the exact `resident_chat_runtime.discord_gateway.GatewayCallbacks` API by inspecting the installed package in the runnable project environment.
2. If it supports typing callbacks, register `on_typing_start`; otherwise, detect `TYPING_START` in `DiscordGatewayBot._run_once` before or around `dispatch_payload`.
3. Add a test for whichever path is implemented: native callback dispatch or raw `TYPING_START` fallback.
4. On typing start, update the pacer’s per-user/channel typing timestamp and do not enqueue a message.
5. Remove the current unconditional `_send_typing_after_delay(...)` task from `_handle_message`; the pacer owns typing UX.
6. Add tests that a partner message is ingested without immediate typing and that typing-start events update pacer state.

### Step 11: Parameterize low-level Discord typing (`app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/messaging.py`, `app/services/agentic.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Change `discord.send_text(to, body)` to accept a keyword such as `send_typing_indicator: bool = True`.
2. Change `send_outbound(...)` to accept a matching Discord typing option and pass it through only for the Discord provider.
3. When an outbound is sent from a paced agentic turn, pass `send_typing_indicator=False` because the pacer already handled typing timing.
4. Leave default low-level typing enabled for non-paced Discord sends such as pause confirmations or operational messages, unless a caller explicitly opts out.
5. Add tests proving paced sends do not call `send_typing` twice and non-paced Discord sends still can emit typing.

### Step 12: Make answer typing feel human (`app/services/pacer.py`, `app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After the pacer chooses `answer`, delay within configured min/max bounds before starting typing.
2. Suppress typing while the user is still typing; if they resume typing during the answer delay, wait until the typing hold expires or the max wait cap is reached.
3. For longer answer generation, optionally refresh Discord typing once using the existing REST typing endpoint; do not loop indefinitely.
4. Record typing wait/started/suppressed facts in `pacing_events.deterministic_signals` or associated reason text.

## Phase 5: Media, Recovery, And Catch-Up Paths

### Step 13: Make non-text media paths explicit (`app/services/transcription.py`, `app/services/vision.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Pass `source="media"` when transcription or vision enqueues a processed inbound message into the coalescer.
2. Ensure media-derived bursts can coalesce with nearby text from the same user, but cannot be pre-turn silenced or reaction-only.
3. Include media indicators in `PacingSignals` so the answer path can tell Phase A that the trigger included voice/image/document context.
4. Add tests where voice/image analysis joins a text burst and produces one answer decision.

### Step 14: Make recovery and catch-up behavior explicit (`app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep `coalescer.add_burst(...)` as direct recovery behavior for known crashed grouped turns.
2. Pass `source="recovery"` for old raw messages recovered by `recover_on_startup(...)`; the pacer should answer without live typing/reaction/silence behavior.
3. Pass `coalescer_source="catch_up"` into `process_inbound(...)` from `catch_up_recent_messages(...)`; do not rely on direct coalescer access in catch-up because catch-up uses the generic inbound processor.
4. Ensure the pacer treats `source="catch_up"` as stale offline history: no reaction-only decision, no silence decision, and no human typing indicators.
5. Add tests proving recovered and catch-up raw messages are not delayed by user-typing state and do not trigger reactions.

## Phase 6: Tests And Docs

### Step 15: Add focused pacing tests (`tests/test_pacer.py`, `tests/test_debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Test deterministic decisions: crisis answers, typing waits, max wait eventually answers, media answers, recovery answers without typing/reaction, catch-up answers without typing/reaction, and unsupported providers use legacy behavior.
2. Test LLM judgement paths with mocked responses, including invalid judgement fallback and spend-cap skip.
3. Test per-user preferences override global timing within bounds.
4. Test observability for wait/react/silence/answer, including signals, reason, preferences snapshot, and source.

### Step 16: Update integration-adjacent tests (`tests/test_discord.py`, `tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_send_outbound.py`, `tests/test_oob_check.py`, `tests/test_whatsapp_webhook.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update Discord tests to assert inbound messages do not emit immediate typing.
2. Add typing-start gateway tests for the verified callback or raw-event path.
3. Add catch-up tests proving `process_inbound(..., coalescer_source="catch_up")` forwards source metadata into the coalescer.
4. Add lifecycle tests proving pacing metadata reaches Phase A and paced outbound disables duplicate low-level typing.
5. Add send-outbound tests for `send_typing_indicator=False` on Discord and default behavior for non-paced sends.
6. Keep OOB tests passing unchanged, proving substantive text still goes through existing outbound guards.
7. Keep WhatsApp webhook tests passing, proving default `coalescer_source="live"` does not alter existing generic inbound behavior.

### Step 17: Document pacing behavior (`README.md` or a focused docs section)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document wait/react/silence/answer decisions, config knobs, per-user preference JSON keys, and defaults.
2. Explain that pre-turn LLM judgement is spend-accounted and disabled when the text spend cap is hit.
3. Add operator tuning guidance for idle/max wait, typing hold, reaction sparseness, disabling pacing, and how catch-up/recovery differ from live messages.

## Execution Order
1. Add settings, per-user preference storage, and `pacing_events` first so behavior has a stable contract.
2. Implement source propagation in `process_inbound(...)` before relying on catch-up/media/recovery source semantics.
3. Implement `app/services/pacer.py` with unit tests before wiring it into live runtime.
4. Add spend accounting for LLM judgement before enabling any LLM pacing path.
5. Extend `BurstCoalescer` with backward-compatible paced callbacks and source metadata.
6. Add `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata(...)` and wire `app/main.py` so production can actually use the pacer.
7. Parameterize Discord typing in `discord.send_text` and `send_outbound` before moving typing ownership to the pacer.
8. Verify and wire gateway typing-start events, then remove immediate inbound typing.
9. Cover media, recovery, and catch-up sources.
10. Finish with docs and broad regression tests.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused unit tests first: `pytest tests/test_pacer.py tests/test_debouncer.py`.
2. Run inbound/source propagation tests: `pytest tests/test_discord.py tests/test_whatsapp_webhook.py`.
3. Run outbound and lifecycle tests: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py`.
4. Run safety tests: `pytest tests/test_oob_check.py`.
5. Run media and recovery tests: `pytest tests/test_recovery.py tests/test_media.py`.
6. Run the full suite with the project’s normal test command once focused checks pass.

  "changes_summary": "Tightened the plan around the remaining catch-up propagation gap: `process_inbound` now explicitly gets a `coalescer_source` parameter, Discord catch-up passes `catch_up` through that generic path, tests prove the source reaches `coalescer.add`, and typing callback verification is promoted into an explicit implementation/test step.",

  "questions": [],

      "requires": [
        "read_files",
        "run_tests",
        "parse_diff"
      ]
    },
    {

      "requires": [
        "run_tests",
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Discord inbound text no longer triggers immediate typing from `_handle_message`; typing is owned by the pacer after an answer decision.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [

        "parse_diff"
      ]
    },

      "criterion": "The implementation verifies and tests either native `GatewayCallbacks` typing support or a raw `TYPING_START` fallback path.",

        "parse_diff"
      ]
    },

      "requires": [

        "parse_diff"
      ]
    },

      ]

      ]

      ]

        "read_files"
      ]

      ]

      "requires": [

        "read_files",
        "parse_diff"
      ]

      "criterion": "Timing and LLM pacing behavior are configurable globally and overridable per user through bounded stored preferences.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "read_files",
        "run_tests",
        "parse_diff"
      ]

      "criterion": "Existing Discord, debouncer, generic inbound, agentic lifecycle, outbound, media, recovery, and OOB tests pass after updates.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },

      "criterion": "Reaction behavior remains sparse, uses a small allowlist, and avoids repeated emoji spam across consecutive routine messages.",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [

      ]
    },

      "criterion": "The pacer module keeps policy logic isolated from Discord transport, coalescer mechanics, and the full agentic lifecycle.",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "read_files",
        "parse_diff",
        "subjective_judgment"
      ]
    },

      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Manual Discord smoke test confirms typing, waiting, reactions, silence, media handling, catch-up behavior, recovery behavior, and answer timing feel natural in a real DM thread.",
      "priority": "info",
      "requires": [
        "verify_physical_device",
        "observe_runtime_logs",
        "subjective_judgment"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "Discord remains the first implementation target; WhatsApp/meta behavior should stay functionally unchanged unless pacing is explicitly enabled for another provider later.",
    "Substantive text must still be generated and delivered only through the existing agentic and OOB-guarded paths.",
    "Per-user preference friendliness for this version means durable JSON preferences on `users`, a resolver that honors them, and tests; it does not require a user-facing preference editor.",
    "Recovered and catch-up messages should not pretend to be live human-paced conversation: no delayed typing, no reaction-only handling, and no silence decisions for stale offline work.",
    "Normal generic inbound processing should default to `coalescer_source=\"live\"`; only explicit callers such as Discord catch-up, recovery, transcription, and vision should override source."
  ],
  "delta_from_previous_percent": 20.14,
  "structure_warnings": []

        Settled decisions (verify the executor implemented these correctly):
- discord-first-scope: Implement pacing for Discord first; keep WhatsApp/meta and other providers on existing behavior unless explicitly enabled later.
- pre-turn-pacer-boundary: The pacer decides wait/react/silence/answer before the agentic turn, but substantive text remains inside the existing agentic and OOB-protected outbound path.
- source-metadata-contract: Every paced coalescer entry carries source metadata; generic inbound defaults to `live`, while catch-up, recovery, transcription, and vision pass explicit sources.
- per-user-preferences: Per-user preference friendliness means bounded JSON preferences stored on `users`, merged with global settings and tested; no user-facing preference editor is required for this version.
- stale-history-behavior: Recovered and catch-up messages do not receive live typing, reaction-only handling, or silence decisions.
- typing-ownership: Paced Discord answers own typing timing in the pacer; low-level Discord sends must support suppressing their default typing indicator to avoid duplication.

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion has `requires` capabilities that are not satisfiable by container workers (e.g., `drive_browser`, `subjective_judgment`), mark it `deferred_human` — NOT `fail` or `waived`. Deferred-human criteria do NOT count toward `needs_rework`.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.
        - The decisions listed above were settled at the gate stage. Verify that the executor implemented each settled decision correctly. Flag deviations from these decisions, but do not question the decisions themselves.
        - baseline_test_failures in finalize.json lists tests that were already failing before execution. Do not flag these as rework items unless the executor introduced new failures in those same tests.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`
          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas

        Idea:
Implement a human-like Discord conversation pacing system for Veas. It should combine deterministic programmatic signals and LLM judgement so the bot feels like a real attentive person rather than message-in/reply-out. Requirements: handle Discord typing signals and pause/extend while user is composing; coalesce multi-message bursts and respond to the whole thought rather than every line; support wait/react/silence/answer decisions before the full agentic turn; use tasteful/elegant emoji/reaction behavior sparingly; avoid over-questioning and compulsive replies; preserve safety/OOB behavior; make typing indicators feel human, including possible stop/start and not typing while the user is typing; add observability/reasoning for why the pacer waited/reacted/silenced/answered; make timing configurable and per-user preference friendly; add tests and docs. Go deep on UX details and implement the best pragmatic version in this repo.

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Human-Like Discord Conversation Pacing

## Overview
Veas already has the right core surfaces: Discord gateway ingestion in `app/services/discord.py`, generic inbound normalization in `app/services/inbound.py`, fixed burst coalescing in `app/services/debouncer.py`, the two-phase agentic lifecycle in `app/services/agentic.py`, outbound/OOB delivery in `app/services/messaging.py`, media enqueue paths in `app/services/transcription.py` and `app/services/vision.py`, startup wiring in `app/main.py`, and recovery in `app/services/recovery.py`.

The latest critique does not show a wrong root cause or wrong architecture. The root cause is still that Discord has fixed burst coalescing plus immediate/provider-level typing, with no pre-turn pacing policy. The remaining concrete gap is source propagation: Discord catch-up enters through `process_inbound(...)`, so `source="catch_up"` will not reach `coalescer.add(...)` unless `process_inbound` accepts and forwards source metadata. The typing callback API also needs implementation-time verification, but that is a bounded Discord transport task rather than a reason to change approach.

The implementation should add a Discord-first pacing layer before the full agentic turn. Deterministic signals handle obvious wait/answer safety cases, a small spend-accounted LLM judgement handles ambiguous routine/notable cases, and substantive text remains in the existing agentic/OOB-protected path. Wait/react/silence/answer decisions get durable observability even when no `bot_turns` row is created.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Configuration, Preferences, Observability

### Step 1: Add global pacing settings (`app/config.py`, `.env.example`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Add conservative Discord pacing settings:
   - `discord_pacing_enabled: bool = True`
   - `discord_pacing_idle_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_max_wait_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_typing_hold_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_min_reply_delay_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_max_reply_delay_seconds`
   - `discord_pacing_llm_enabled: bool = True`
   - `discord_pacing_model`, defaulting to the existing cheap judgement model unless product tuning chooses otherwise.
2. Gate behavior on `messaging_provider == "discord"` so WhatsApp/meta behavior stays unchanged.
3. Add bounded min/max validation in code so per-user overrides cannot create extreme sleeps.

### Step 2: Add per-user pacing preferences (`migrations/0008_discord_pacing.sql`, `app/models/user.py`, `tests/conftest.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `users.pacing_preferences jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb` in the new migration.
2. Define supported preference keys in code: `idle_seconds`, `max_wait_seconds`, `typing_hold_seconds`, `llm_pacing_enabled`, `reaction_style`, and `answer_bias`.
3. Implement a resolver that merges global settings with `users.pacing_preferences`, clamps unsafe values, and falls back cleanly for users with `{}`.
4. Update `FakePool` user rows and user fetch helpers so tests can store and read pacing preferences.

### Step 3: Create durable pacing observability (`migrations/0008_discord_pacing.sql`, `tests/conftest.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a `pacing_events` table with `user_id`, `message_ids uuid[]`, `provider`, `decision`, `reason`, `deterministic_signals jsonb`, `llm_judgment jsonb`, `preferences_snapshot jsonb`, `source`, `created_at`, and optional `acted_at`.
2. Add indexes for recent events by user/time and message ids.
3. Update `FakePool` to support inserting pacing events and reading them in tests.
4. Record events for every terminal decision (`react`, `silence`, `answer`) and meaningful waits/extensions.

## Phase 2: Pacer Core And Cost Controls

### Step 4: Implement the pacer module (`app/services/pacer.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Define `PacingDecision` values: `wait`, `react`, `silence`, and `answer`.
2. Define `PacingSignals` from real message rows and runtime state: burst size, elapsed time, last inbound age, max charge, current Discord typing state, source (`live`, `media`, `recovery`, `catch_up`), media presence, question/direct-request indicators, fragment-like content, repeat reactions, and provider.
3. Fetch message rows by `message_ids` inside the pacer so text, charge, media type, and stale/recovered status are based on persisted data rather than caller guesses.
4. Add deterministic hard gates:
   - `/pause` and `/resume` remain handled before pacing in `app/services/inbound.py`.
   - `crisis` always answers through the full agentic path, with no silence or reaction-only decision.
   - `charged` may wait briefly for continuation but cannot be reaction-only; silence requires explicit LLM justification and a recorded event.
   - Media-derived messages from transcription/vision answer after coalescing and should not be pre-turn reacted/silenced.
   - Recovered or catch-up messages bypass human typing/reaction behavior and answer immediately or enter the existing recovery flow.
   - If Discord says the user is typing, wait/extend up to the configured cap.
5. Enforce reaction taste in code: small allowlist, cooldown by recent `pacing_events`, no reactions for crisis/media/recovered/catch-up messages, and no repeated reaction-only responses in a short window.

### Step 5: Add spend-accounted LLM pacing judgement (`app/services/pacer.py`, `app/services/spend.py` or a shared cost helper)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Before any pre-turn LLM call, check `is_under_cap(pool, "text")`; if not under cap, skip LLM pacing and choose deterministic `answer` or defer only through the existing spend-cap behavior.
2. After the LLM judgement call, record cost with the same pricing rules used by agentic turns. Prefer extracting a shared helper from `app/services/agentic.py` rather than duplicating private cost math.
3. Use a JSON-only prompt for ambiguous routine/notable live Discord bursts. Allowed output fields: `decision`, `reason`, `emoji`, and `confidence`.
4. Validate the LLM decision against deterministic gates before acting. Invalid or unsafe judgement falls back to `answer` and records the fallback reason.

## Phase 3: Source Propagation, Coalescer, And Agentic Metadata

### Step 6: Add source metadata to inbound processing (`app/services/inbound.py`, `app/services/discord.py`, tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `process_inbound(pool, payload, coalescer=None)` with a keyword-only parameter such as `coalescer_source: str = "live"`.
2. Forward `coalescer_source` into every text/document coalescer enqueue as `await coalescer.add(user.id, row["id"], user, source=coalescer_source)`.
3. Keep the default as `"live"` so WhatsApp webhooks, Twilio webhooks, and normal Discord gateway messages preserve current behavior without caller changes.
4. In `app/services/discord.py`, call `process_inbound(..., coalescer_source="catch_up")` from `catch_up_recent_messages(...)`.
5. Add tests proving catch-up ingestion through `process_inbound(...)` reaches the coalescer with `source="catch_up"`, while normal inbound calls default to `source="live"`.

### Step 7: Extend burst coalescing without breaking legacy callers (`app/services/debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep the existing `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)` path intact for tests and non-paced use.
2. Add optional constructor dependencies: `pacer`, `on_paced_burst_complete`, and default `source="live"` support for `add(...)`.
3. Keep `add(user_id, message_id, user)` backward compatible; add optional keyword metadata such as `source="live"` so inbound, recovery, media, and catch-up callers can be explicit.
4. Store source metadata per burst. If multiple sources join one burst, preserve the highest-safety source in order such as `recovery`/`catch_up` over `media` over `live`.
5. On idle expiry, if no pacer is configured, call the legacy callback exactly as today.
6. If a pacer is configured, ask it for a decision. For `wait`, reschedule the same burst without losing message ids. For `answer`, call `on_paced_burst_complete(message_ids, user, pacing_metadata=...)`. For `react`/`silence`, mark messages processed and do not run the full agentic turn.
7. Preserve `add_burst(...)` as direct callback behavior for crash recovery of already-formed batches.

### Step 8: Add an agentic metadata entry point (`app/services/agentic.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata(triggering_message_ids, user, *, pacing_metadata)` as the explicit callback target for paced answers.
2. Delegate to `_run_agentic(..., trigger_metadata={"kind": "inbound", "pacing": pacing_metadata})` so Phase A sees why the pacer answered.
3. Keep `run_agentic_turn(...)` unchanged for current callers.
4. In the Phase A seed, include compact pacing context: decision reason, wait count/duration, user typing stopped/expired, burst size, source, and any preference override that mattered.
5. Tighten prompt language around whole-burst response, avoiding over-questioning, sparse reactions, and acceptable silence while preserving the existing OOB rules.

### Step 9: Wire the live app construction (`app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Construct a `DiscordPacer` or equivalent only when `messaging_provider == "discord"` and pacing is enabled.
2. Pass `pool`, `settings`, the pacer, and `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata` into `BurstCoalescer` during startup.
3. Store the pacer on `app.state.discord_pacer` so `DiscordGatewayBot`, recovery, and tests can share typing state.
4. Keep the old `BurstCoalescer(on_burst_complete=run_agentic_turn)` construction for non-Discord or pacing-disabled providers.
5. Add startup tests or app-lifespan tests proving the production construction actually attaches the pacer for Discord.

## Phase 4: Discord Typing And Delivery

### Step 10: Verify and wire Discord typing-start state (`app/services/discord.py`, `tests/test_discord.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. During implementation, verify the exact `resident_chat_runtime.discord_gateway.GatewayCallbacks` API by inspecting the installed package in the runnable project environment.
2. If it supports typing callbacks, register `on_typing_start`; otherwise, detect `TYPING_START` in `DiscordGatewayBot._run_once` before or around `dispatch_payload`.
3. Add a test for whichever path is implemented: native callback dispatch or raw `TYPING_START` fallback.
4. On typing start, update the pacer’s per-user/channel typing timestamp and do not enqueue a message.
5. Remove the current unconditional `_send_typing_after_delay(...)` task from `_handle_message`; the pacer owns typing UX.
6. Add tests that a partner message is ingested without immediate typing and that typing-start events update pacer state.

### Step 11: Parameterize low-level Discord typing (`app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/messaging.py`, `app/services/agentic.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Change `discord.send_text(to, body)` to accept a keyword such as `send_typing_indicator: bool = True`.
2. Change `send_outbound(...)` to accept a matching Discord typing option and pass it through only for the Discord provider.
3. When an outbound is sent from a paced agentic turn, pass `send_typing_indicator=False` because the pacer already handled typing timing.
4. Leave default low-level typing enabled for non-paced Discord sends such as pause confirmations or operational messages, unless a caller explicitly opts out.
5. Add tests proving paced sends do not call `send_typing` twice and non-paced Discord sends still can emit typing.

### Step 12: Make answer typing feel human (`app/services/pacer.py`, `app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After the pacer chooses `answer`, delay within configured min/max bounds before starting typing.
2. Suppress typing while the user is still typing; if they resume typing during the answer delay, wait until the typing hold expires or the max wait cap is reached.
3. For longer answer generation, optionally refresh Discord typing once using the existing REST typing endpoint; do not loop indefinitely.
4. Record typing wait/started/suppressed facts in `pacing_events.deterministic_signals` or associated reason text.

## Phase 5: Media, Recovery, And Catch-Up Paths

### Step 13: Make non-text media paths explicit (`app/services/transcription.py`, `app/services/vision.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Pass `source="media"` when transcription or vision enqueues a processed inbound message into the coalescer.
2. Ensure media-derived bursts can coalesce with nearby text from the same user, but cannot be pre-turn silenced or reaction-only.
3. Include media indicators in `PacingSignals` so the answer path can tell Phase A that the trigger included voice/image/document context.
4. Add tests where voice/image analysis joins a text burst and produces one answer decision.

### Step 14: Make recovery and catch-up behavior explicit (`app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep `coalescer.add_burst(...)` as direct recovery behavior for known crashed grouped turns.
2. Pass `source="recovery"` for old raw messages recovered by `recover_on_startup(...)`; the pacer should answer without live typing/reaction/silence behavior.
3. Pass `coalescer_source="catch_up"` into `process_inbound(...)` from `catch_up_recent_messages(...)`; do not rely on direct coalescer access in catch-up because catch-up uses the generic inbound processor.
4. Ensure the pacer treats `source="catch_up"` as stale offline history: no reaction-only decision, no silence decision, and no human typing indicators.
5. Add tests proving recovered and catch-up raw messages are not delayed by user-typing state and do not trigger reactions.

## Phase 6: Tests And Docs

### Step 15: Add focused pacing tests (`tests/test_pacer.py`, `tests/test_debouncer.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Test deterministic decisions: crisis answers, typing waits, max wait eventually answers, media answers, recovery answers without typing/reaction, catch-up answers without typing/reaction, and unsupported providers use legacy behavior.
2. Test LLM judgement paths with mocked responses, including invalid judgement fallback and spend-cap skip.
3. Test per-user preferences override global timing within bounds.
4. Test observability for wait/react/silence/answer, including signals, reason, preferences snapshot, and source.

### Step 16: Update integration-adjacent tests (`tests/test_discord.py`, `tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_send_outbound.py`, `tests/test_oob_check.py`, `tests/test_whatsapp_webhook.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update Discord tests to assert inbound messages do not emit immediate typing.
2. Add typing-start gateway tests for the verified callback or raw-event path.
3. Add catch-up tests proving `process_inbound(..., coalescer_source="catch_up")` forwards source metadata into the coalescer.
4. Add lifecycle tests proving pacing metadata reaches Phase A and paced outbound disables duplicate low-level typing.
5. Add send-outbound tests for `send_typing_indicator=False` on Discord and default behavior for non-paced sends.
6. Keep OOB tests passing unchanged, proving substantive text still goes through existing outbound guards.
7. Keep WhatsApp webhook tests passing, proving default `coalescer_source="live"` does not alter existing generic inbound behavior.

### Step 17: Document pacing behavior (`README.md` or a focused docs section)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document wait/react/silence/answer decisions, config knobs, per-user preference JSON keys, and defaults.
2. Explain that pre-turn LLM judgement is spend-accounted and disabled when the text spend cap is hit.
3. Add operator tuning guidance for idle/max wait, typing hold, reaction sparseness, disabling pacing, and how catch-up/recovery differ from live messages.

## Execution Order
1. Add settings, per-user preference storage, and `pacing_events` first so behavior has a stable contract.
2. Implement source propagation in `process_inbound(...)` before relying on catch-up/media/recovery source semantics.
3. Implement `app/services/pacer.py` with unit tests before wiring it into live runtime.
4. Add spend accounting for LLM judgement before enabling any LLM pacing path.
5. Extend `BurstCoalescer` with backward-compatible paced callbacks and source metadata.
6. Add `run_agentic_turn_with_metadata(...)` and wire `app/main.py` so production can actually use the pacer.
7. Parameterize Discord typing in `discord.send_text` and `send_outbound` before moving typing ownership to the pacer.
8. Verify and wire gateway typing-start events, then remove immediate inbound typing.
9. Cover media, recovery, and catch-up sources.
10. Finish with docs and broad regression tests.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused unit tests first: `pytest tests/test_pacer.py tests/test_debouncer.py`.
2. Run inbound/source propagation tests: `pytest tests/test_discord.py tests/test_whatsapp_webhook.py`.
3. Run outbound and lifecycle tests: `pytest tests/test_send_outbound.py tests/test_agentic_lifecycle.py`.
4. Run safety tests: `pytest tests/test_oob_check.py`.
5. Run media and recovery tests: `pytest tests/test_recovery.py tests/test_media.py`.
6. Run the full suite with the project’s normal test command once focused checks pass.

      "id": "typing-ownership",

  "signals": {
    "iteration": 3,
    "idea": "Implement a human-like Discord conversation pacing system for Veas. It should combine deterministic programmatic signals and LLM judgement so the bot feels like a real attentive person rather than message-in/reply-out. Requirements: handle Discord typing signals and pause/extend while user is composing; coalesce multi-message bursts and respond to the whole thought rather than every line; support wait/react/silence/answer decisions before the full agentic turn; use tasteful/elegant emoji/reaction behavior sparingly; avoid over-questioning and compulsive replies; preserve safety/OOB behavior; make typing indicators feel human, including possible stop/start and not typing while the user is typing; add observability/reasoning for why the pacer waited/reacted/silenced/answered; make timing configurable and per-user preference friendly; add tests and docs. Go deep on UX details and implement the best pragmatic version in this repo.",

        Settled decisions (verify the executor implemented these correctly):
- discord-first-scope: Implement pacing for Discord first; keep WhatsApp/meta and other providers on existing behavior unless explicitly enabled later.
- pre-turn-pacer-boundary: The pacer decides wait/react/silence/answer before the agentic turn, but substantive text remains inside the existing agentic and OOB-protected outbound path.
- source-metadata-contract: Every paced coalescer entry carries source metadata; generic inbound defaults to `live`, while catch-up, recovery, transcription, and vision pass explicit sources.
- per-user-preferences: Per-user preference friendliness means bounded JSON preferences stored on `users`, merged with global settings and tested; no user-facing preference editor is required for this version.
- stale-history-behavior: Recovered and catch-up messages do not receive live typing, reaction-only handling, or silence decisions.
- typing-ownership: Paced Discord answers own typing timing in the pacer; low-level Discord sends must support suppressing their default typing indicator to avoid duplication.

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion has `requires` capabilities that are not satisfiable by container workers (e.g., `drive_browser`, `subjective_judgment`), mark it `deferred_human` — NOT `fail` or `waived`. Deferred-human criteria do NOT count toward `needs_rework`.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.
        - The decisions listed above were settled at the gate stage. Verify that the executor implemented each settled decision correctly. Flag deviations from these decisions, but do not question the decisions themselves.
        - baseline_test_failures in finalize.json lists tests that were already failing before execution. Do not flag these as rework items unless the executor introduced new failures in those same tests.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`
          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.
